Deuteronomy cross-references

Deuteronomy 1:1–16:22 · 472 completed verses · 555 completed pairs

Initial curated pass. Next: Deuteronomy 17:1. Later source verses remain pending. Greek-based Saints-Translation numbering is retained.

Completed means the initial curated cross-reference pass: the source verse and every listed target passage were read in the pinned Saints-Translation English records, and each retained pair has a specific explanation. It does not mean every possible biblical connection has been exhausted, or independent scholarly approval.

Deuteronomy 1:1

These are the words which Moses spoke unto all Israel beyond the Jordan, in the wilderness toward the west, near the Red Sea, between Paran, Tophel, Laban, Aulon, and Katachrysea.

  1. Deuteronomy 1:5 Same-chapter discourse setting

    The opening locates Moses beyond the Jordan, and the later introductory notice specifies Moab as the place where he explained the law; the additional geographical names are not silently equated with other sites.

    Read matching passage

    Deuteronomy 1:5 beyond the Jordan, in the land of Moab, Moses began to make this law plain, saying:

    Pinned source record

Deuteronomy 1:2

It is a journey of eleven days from Horeb by the way of Mount Seir unto Kadesh-barnea.

  1. Deuteronomy 1:19 Same-chapter route and narrated arrival

    The header states an eleven-day journey from Horeb by Mount Seir to Kadesh-barnea; the recital subsequently describes departure from Horeb and arrival at Kadesh-barnea, without saying the whole narrated interval lasted eleven days.

    Read matching passage

    Deuteronomy 1:19 And we departed from Horeb, and went through all that great and fearful wilderness which you saw, by the way of the hill country of the Amorite, as the Lord our God commanded us; and we came unto Kadesh-barnea.

    Pinned source record

Deuteronomy 1:3

And it came to pass in the fortieth year, in the eleventh month, on the first day of the month, that Moses spoke unto all the sons of Israel, according to all that the Lord had commanded him concerning them.

  1. Numbers 33:38 Fortieth-year chronological notices

    Moses speaks on the first day of the eleventh month in the fortieth year; Aaron dies on the first day of the fifth month in that year. These are distinct dated events, not identical dates.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 33:38 And Aaron the priest went up by the Lord’s command and died there in the fortieth year of the departure of the sons of Israel from the land of Egypt, in the fifth month, on the first day of the month.

    Pinned source record

Deuteronomy 1:4

After he had smitten Sihon king of the Amorites, who dwelt in Heshbon, and Og king of Bashan, who dwelt in Ashtaroth and in Edrei,

  1. Numbers 21:23-25 Sihon conquest recalled

    The discourse follows the defeat of Sihon of Heshbon; Numbers narrates Sihon refusing passage, Israel defeating him, and Israel taking the Amorite towns including Heshbon.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 21:23 And Sihon would not allow Israel to pass through his territory. He gathered all his people and went out into the wilderness to array himself against Israel. He came to Jahaz and arrayed himself against Israel.

    Numbers 21:24 And Israel struck him by slaughter with the sword, and they took control of his land from the Arnon to the Jabbok, as far as the sons of Ammon, because Jazer is the border of the sons of Ammon.

    Numbers 21:25 And Israel took all these towns and settled in all the Amorite towns, in Heshbon and all the towns adjoining it.

    Pinned source record

  2. Numbers 21:33-35 Og conquest recalled

    The introductory notice names the defeat of Og of Bashan associated with Ashtaroth and Edrei; Numbers narrates Og coming to battle at Edrei and the defeat of him, his sons, and his people.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 21:33 And they turned and went up by the road to Bashan. Og king of Bashan came out to meet them with all his people, for battle at Edrei.

    Numbers 21:34 And the Lord said unto Moses: Do not fear him, for I have handed him, all his people, and all his land into your hands. You shall do to him as you did to Sihon king of the Amorites, who lived in Heshbon.

    Numbers 21:35 And he struck him, and his sons, and all his people, until he left none of his alive; and they inherited their land.

    Pinned source record

Deuteronomy 1:5

beyond the Jordan, in the land of Moab, Moses began to make this law plain, saying:

  1. Deuteronomy 4:44-46 Repeated law-discourse frame

    Moses explains the law beyond the Jordan in Moab; the later frame again identifies Moses setting forth the law beyond the Jordan, in the ravine near the house of Peor and Sihon’s former territory.

    Read matching passage

    Deuteronomy 4:44 This is the law which Moses set before the sons of Israel.

    Deuteronomy 4:45 These are the testimonies, and the ordinances, and the judgments which Moses spoke unto the sons of Israel in the wilderness, when they came forth out of the land of Egypt,

    Deuteronomy 4:46 beyond the Jordan, in the ravine near the house of Peor, in the land of Sihon king of the Amorites, who dwelt in Heshbon, whom Moses and the sons of Israel smote when they came forth out of the land of Egypt.

    Pinned source record

Deuteronomy 1:6

The Lord our God spoke unto us in Horeb, saying: Let it suffice you to have dwelt in this mountain.

  1. Deuteronomy 1:19 Same-chapter departure command and execution

    The command says that the stay at Horeb has been sufficient; Moses subsequently recounts the actual departure from Horeb and journey through the fearful wilderness to Kadesh-barnea.

    Read matching passage

    Deuteronomy 1:19 And we departed from Horeb, and went through all that great and fearful wilderness which you saw, by the way of the hill country of the Amorite, as the Lord our God commanded us; and we came unto Kadesh-barnea.

    Pinned source record

Deuteronomy 1:7

Turn and set forth, and enter the hill country of the Amorites and all the neighbouring places of Arabah, the hill country and the plain, the southwest and the seacoast, the land of the Canaanites and Anti-Lebanon, as far as the great river Euphrates.

  1. Deuteronomy 1:19-21 Same-chapter commanded destination and arrival

    The command directs Israel toward the Amorite hill country among the wider named regions; the recital then reports arrival at that hill country and commands possession. It does not claim every region extending to the Euphrates was already occupied.

    Read matching passage

    Deuteronomy 1:19 And we departed from Horeb, and went through all that great and fearful wilderness which you saw, by the way of the hill country of the Amorite, as the Lord our God commanded us; and we came unto Kadesh-barnea.

    Deuteronomy 1:20 And I said unto you: You have come unto the hill country of the Amorite, which the Lord our God gives you.

    Deuteronomy 1:21 Behold, the Lord your God has delivered the land before you. Go up and inherit it, as the Lord, the God of your fathers, has spoken unto you; fear not, neither be dismayed.

    Pinned source record

Deuteronomy 1:8

Behold, I have delivered the land before you. Enter and inherit the land which I sware unto your fathers, Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob, to give unto them and unto their seed after them.

  1. Exodus 6:8 Patriarchal land oath and inheritance

    Both passages name Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob in the Lord’s undertaking to give their land to Israel as an inheritance; Deuteronomy urges possession of the land promised in the earlier assurance.

    Read matching passage

    Exodus 6:8 I will bring you into the land concerning which I stretched out My hand to give it to Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob. I will give it to you as an inheritance. I am the Lord.’”

    Pinned source record

Deuteronomy 1:9

And I spoke unto you at that time, saying: I shall not be able to bear you alone.

  1. Numbers 11:14 Moses unable to bear the people alone

    Moses’ recollection that he could not bear Israel alone corresponds to his explicit complaint that the people’s burden was too heavy; Numbers places that complaint in the meat-demand episode.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 11:14 I shall not be able to carry this people alone, because this matter is too heavy for me.

    Pinned source record

  2. Exodus 18:18 Judicial burden requiring assistance

    The recollection of inability to bear the people alone matches Jethro’s specific warning that judging the people alone would exhaust Moses and the people. The passages retain their different speakers.

    Read matching passage

    Exodus 18:18 You shall be worn out with unbearable exhaustion, both you and all this people who are with you; this matter is heavy for you, and you cannot do it alone.

    Pinned source record

Deuteronomy 1:10

The Lord your God has multiplied you, and behold, you are this day as the stars of heaven in multitude.

  1. Genesis 15:5 Descendants compared with heavenly stars

    Moses describes Israel’s present multiplication as the stars of heaven; the earlier promise directs Abram to count the stars and says his seed will be like them.

    Read matching passage

    Genesis 15:5 And He brought him outside and said to him, “Look now toward heaven, and count the stars, if you can count them.” And He said, “So shall your seed be.”

    Pinned source record

  2. Genesis 22:17 Star-numbered descendants and blessing

    Israel’s multiplication like the heavenly stars recalls the promise to bless Abraham and multiply his seed like the stars; this comparison does not claim an exact numerical census of the stars.

    Read matching passage

    Genesis 22:17 surely I will bless you, and greatly multiply your seed as the stars of the heaven, and as the sand upon the shore of the sea; and your seed shall inherit the cities of their adversaries.

    Pinned source record

Deuteronomy 1:11

The Lord, the God of your fathers, make you a thousand times as many as you are, and bless you, as He has spoken unto you.

  1. Genesis 22:17-18 Prayer for continued ancestral blessing

    Moses asks the God of the fathers to multiply and bless Israel; the ancestral promise joins blessing with multiplication of Abraham’s seed. The requested thousandfold increase belongs to Moses’ prayer, not to an identical number in Genesis.

    Read matching passage

    Genesis 22:17 surely I will bless you, and greatly multiply your seed as the stars of the heaven, and as the sand upon the shore of the sea; and your seed shall inherit the cities of their adversaries.

    Genesis 22:18 And in your seed all the nations of the earth shall be blessed, because you have listened to My voice.”

    Pinned source record

Deuteronomy 1:12

How shall I alone be able to bear your trouble, and your resistance, and your disputes?

  1. Exodus 18:18-22 Burden of disputes shared with appointed judges

    Moses asks how he can bear the people’s trouble and disputes alone; Jethro identifies the heavy judicial burden and proposes subordinate judges who will bear it with him.

    Read matching passage

    Exodus 18:18 You shall be worn out with unbearable exhaustion, both you and all this people who are with you; this matter is heavy for you, and you cannot do it alone.

    Exodus 18:19 Now therefore listen to me, and I will counsel you, and God shall be with you. Represent the people in matters pertaining to God, and bring their words to God.

    Exodus 18:20 And you shall solemnly instruct them in the ordinances of God and His law, and show them the ways in which they shall walk, and the works which they shall do.

    Exodus 18:21 And choose for yourself from all the people able men who fear God, righteous men who hate arrogance; and you shall set them over the people as rulers of thousands, and rulers of hundreds, and rulers of fifties, and rulers of tens.

    Exodus 18:22 And they shall judge the people at all times; the weighty matter they shall bring to you, but the lesser judgments they shall judge themselves; and they shall lighten your burden, and bear it with you.

    Pinned source record

Deuteronomy 1:13

Choose for yourselves wise men, men of knowledge and understanding, for your tribes, and I will appoint them to rule over you.

  1. Exodus 18:21 Selection of qualified tribal leaders

    Both passages require qualified men to assist government, but preserve their selection procedures: Moses asks the people to supply wise and understanding men by tribes, while Jethro tells Moses to choose able, God-fearing, righteous men.

    Read matching passage

    Exodus 18:21 And choose for yourself from all the people able men who fear God, righteous men who hate arrogance; and you shall set them over the people as rulers of thousands, and rulers of hundreds, and rulers of fifties, and rulers of tens.

    Pinned source record

Deuteronomy 1:14

And you answered me and said: The thing which you have spoken is good to do.

  1. Deuteronomy 1:13 Same-chapter proposal and public approval

    The people’s declaration that the proposed course is good answers the immediately specified proposal to supply wise and understanding tribal men for Moses to appoint as leaders.

    Read matching passage

    Deuteronomy 1:13 Choose for yourselves wise men, men of knowledge and understanding, for your tribes, and I will appoint them to rule over you.

    Pinned source record

Deuteronomy 1:15

And I took from among you wise men, men of knowledge and understanding, and appointed them to rule over you, captains of thousands, and captains of hundreds, and captains of fifties, and captains of tens, and recording officers for your judges.

  1. Exodus 18:25 Matching graded leadership ranks

    Both appointments specify leaders of thousands, hundreds, fifties, and tens; Deuteronomy additionally mentions recording officers for the judges, without inserting that additional office into Exodus.

    Read matching passage

    Exodus 18:25 Moses chose capable men from all Israel and appointed them over the people as leaders of thousands, hundreds, fifties, and tens.

    Pinned source record

Deuteronomy 1:16

And I commanded your judges at that time, saying: Hear the matters between your brethren, and judge justly between a man and his brother and the sojourner with him.

  1. Leviticus 24:22 Impartial judgment for native and foreigner

    The judges must hear brothers and judge righteously between a man, his brother, and his resident foreigner; Leviticus explicitly requires one judgment for resident foreigner and local inhabitant.

    Read matching passage

    Leviticus 24:22 There shall be one judgment for the resident foreigner and the local inhabitant, for I am the Lord your God.

    Pinned source record

Deuteronomy 1:17

You shall not show partiality in judgment; you shall judge the small and the great alike. You shall not shrink before the face of man, for judgment belongs unto God. And you shall bring unto me the matter which is too hard for you, and I will hear it.

  1. Leviticus 19:15 Judgment without social partiality

    Hearing small and great alike corresponds to the law against favouring the poor or admiring the mighty in judgment; both address concrete impartiality in deciding cases.

    Read matching passage

    Leviticus 19:15 You shall do no injustice in judgment; you shall not favour the person of the poor, neither shall you admire the person of the mighty; you shall judge your neighbour in righteousness.

    Pinned source record

  2. Exodus 18:22 Difficult cases reserved for Moses

    The instruction to bring a difficult matter to Moses matches Jethro’s arrangement that subordinate judges handle lesser judgments while weighty matters come to Moses.

    Read matching passage

    Exodus 18:22 And they shall judge the people at all times; the weighty matter they shall bring to you, but the lesser judgments they shall judge themselves; and they shall lighten your burden, and bear it with you.

    Pinned source record

Deuteronomy 1:18

And I commanded you at that time all the things which you should do.

  1. Deuteronomy 1:16-17 Same-chapter judicial summary and its contents

    The notice that Moses commanded the required conduct summarizes the preceding instructions to the judges about righteous hearings, equal attention to small and great, and referral of difficult cases.

    Read matching passage

    Deuteronomy 1:16 And I commanded your judges at that time, saying: Hear the matters between your brethren, and judge justly between a man and his brother and the sojourner with him.

    Deuteronomy 1:17 You shall not show partiality in judgment; you shall judge the small and the great alike. You shall not shrink before the face of man, for judgment belongs unto God. And you shall bring unto me the matter which is too hard for you, and I will hear it.

    Pinned source record

Deuteronomy 1:19

And we departed from Horeb, and went through all that great and fearful wilderness which you saw, by the way of the hill country of the Amorite, as the Lord our God commanded us; and we came unto Kadesh-barnea.

  1. Deuteronomy 1:6-7 Same-chapter departure follows stated route command

    The report of leaving Horeb and approaching the Amorite hill country follows the command to leave the mountain and journey toward that country; Kadesh-barnea is the reported arrival point.

    Read matching passage

    Deuteronomy 1:6 The Lord our God spoke unto us in Horeb, saying: Let it suffice you to have dwelt in this mountain.

    Deuteronomy 1:7 Turn and set forth, and enter the hill country of the Amorites and all the neighbouring places of Arabah, the hill country and the plain, the southwest and the seacoast, the land of the Canaanites and Anti-Lebanon, as far as the great river Euphrates.

    Pinned source record

Deuteronomy 1:20

And I said unto you: You have come unto the hill country of the Amorite, which the Lord our God gives you.

  1. Deuteronomy 1:7 Same-chapter destination identified on arrival

    Moses identifies the reached country as the Amorite hill country given by God; that region had already been named explicitly in the departure command, alongside wider geographical destinations.

    Read matching passage

    Deuteronomy 1:7 Turn and set forth, and enter the hill country of the Amorites and all the neighbouring places of Arabah, the hill country and the plain, the southwest and the seacoast, the land of the Canaanites and Anti-Lebanon, as far as the great river Euphrates.

    Pinned source record

Deuteronomy 1:21

Behold, the Lord your God has delivered the land before you. Go up and inherit it, as the Lord, the God of your fathers, has spoken unto you; fear not, neither be dismayed.

  1. Numbers 14:8-9 Exhortations to possess the land without fear

    Moses commands Israel to inherit the land and not fear; Joshua and Caleb likewise urge confidence that the Lord will give the land and prohibit fear of its people. The exhortations retain their different speakers.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 14:8 If the Lord chooses us, He will bring us into this land and give it to us, a land flowing with milk and honey.

    Numbers 14:9 Only do not become deserters from the Lord. Do not fear the people of the land, for they are food for us. Their season has passed away from them, but the Lord is among us. Do not fear them.

    Pinned source record

Deuteronomy 1:22

And you all came unto me, and said: Let us send men before us, and let them search out the land for us, and bring us word of the way by which we shall go up, and of the cities into which we shall enter.

  1. Numbers 13:1-3 Differently framed commissioning of the scouts

    Deuteronomy recalls the people proposing scouts to report the route and cities; Numbers gives the Lord’s command to Moses and his sending of tribal leaders. Neither account’s initiating speaker is replaced with the other’s.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 13:1 And the Lord spoke unto Moses, saying:

    Numbers 13:2 Send men for yourself, and let them search out the land of the Canaanites, which I give unto the sons of Israel for a possession. You shall send one man from each tribe, according to the groups of their ancestral families, each a leader among them.

    Numbers 13:3 And Moses sent them from the wilderness of Paran through the Lord’s voice. All these men were leaders of the sons of Israel.

    Pinned source record

Deuteronomy 1:23

And the saying pleased me; and I took twelve men from among you, one man from each tribe.

  1. Numbers 13:2 One representative from each tribe

    Moses recalls choosing twelve men, one from each tribe; the Lord’s scouting command requires one man from each tribe, each a leader. No individual name equivalence is inferred from this shared arrangement.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 13:2 Send men for yourself, and let them search out the land of the Canaanites, which I give unto the sons of Israel for a possession. You shall send one man from each tribe, according to the groups of their ancestral families, each a leader among them.

    Pinned source record

Deuteronomy 1:24

And they turned and went up into the hill country, and came unto the Valley of the Cluster, and searched it out.

  1. Numbers 13:23-24 Scouting visit to the cluster-named valley

    The scouts ascend and reach the Valley of the Cluster; Numbers describes their visit to the Ravine of the Cluster, its fruit, and its naming. The distinct valley and ravine renderings are retained.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 13:23 And they came as far as the Ravine of the Cluster and inspected it. They cut from there a branch with one cluster of grapes upon it and carried it on carrying poles, with some pomegranates and figs.

    Numbers 13:24 They named that place Ravine of the Cluster because of the cluster the sons of Israel cut from there.

    Pinned source record

Deuteronomy 1:25

And they took of the fruit of the land in their hands, and brought it down unto us, and said: The land which the Lord our God gives us is good.

  1. Numbers 13:26-27 Fruit brought back and favourable land report

    The men bring fruit and report that the land God gives is good; Numbers records the returned scouts displaying fruit and describing the land as flowing with milk and honey.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 13:26 And they came unto Moses, Aaron, and the whole congregation of the sons of Israel in the wilderness of Paran, Kadesh. They gave a report to them and the whole congregation and showed the land’s fruit.

    Numbers 13:27 And they told him, and said: We came unto the land to which you sent us, a land flowing with milk and honey; and this is its fruit.

    Pinned source record

Deuteronomy 1:26

And you would not go up, but disobeyed the word of the Lord your God.

  1. Numbers 14:1-4 Refusal following the scouting report

    Moses recounts Israel refusing to ascend and resisting God’s command; Numbers gives the congregation’s weeping, grumbling, and proposal to appoint a leader and return to Egypt.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 14:1 And the whole congregation raised its voice, and the people wept all that night.

    Numbers 14:2 And all the sons of Israel grumbled against Moses and Aaron. The whole congregation said to them: If only we had died in the land of Egypt! Or if only we had died in this wilderness!

    Numbers 14:3 And why is the Lord bringing us into this land to fall in war? Our wives and children will become plunder. So now it is better for us to return to Egypt.

    Numbers 14:4 And they said to one another, Let us appoint a leader and return to Egypt.

    Pinned source record

Deuteronomy 1:27

And you murmured in your tents, and said: Because the Lord hates us, He brought us forth out of the land of Egypt, to deliver us into the hands of the Amorites, to destroy us.

  1. Numbers 14:2-3 Accusations about the purpose of the exodus

    The people accuse the Lord of bringing them from Egypt through hatred to destruction by the Amorites; Numbers reports their complaint that entering the land will mean death in war. These remain the people’s accusations.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 14:2 And all the sons of Israel grumbled against Moses and Aaron. The whole congregation said to them: If only we had died in the land of Egypt! Or if only we had died in this wilderness!

    Numbers 14:3 And why is the Lord bringing us into this land to fall in war? Our wives and children will become plunder. So now it is better for us to return to Egypt.

    Pinned source record

Deuteronomy 1:28

Whither do we go up? Your brethren have made your heart fail, saying: The nation is great and numerous and mightier than we; and the cities are great and walled up to heaven. Moreover, we have seen sons of giants there.

  1. Numbers 13:28-33 Fear-producing report of strong inhabitants

    The recital names stronger people, great walled cities, and giants as the discouraging report; Numbers similarly describes strongly fortified cities, Anak’s family, stronger inhabitants, and giants.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 13:28 But the nation living on it is bold, and the cities are strongly fortified with walls and very large. We saw the family of Anak there.

    Numbers 13:29 And Amalek dwells in the land toward the south; and the Hittite, and the Hivite, and the Jebusite, and the Amorite dwell in the hill country; and the Canaanite dwells beside the sea and beside the river Jordan.

    Numbers 13:30 And Caleb quieted the people before Moses and said to him: No! We shall certainly go up and inherit it, for we shall surely prevail against them.

    Numbers 13:31 And the men who had gone up with him said: We are not going up, for we shall certainly not be able to go up against the nation, because it is much stronger than we are.

    Numbers 13:32 And they brought dismay concerning the land which they had searched out unto the sons of Israel, saying: The land through which we passed to search it out is a land that devours its inhabitants; all the people whom we saw therein are men of exceeding height;

    Numbers 13:33 And there we saw the giants. We were like grasshoppers before them, and indeed so we were before them.

    Pinned source record

Deuteronomy 1:29

And I said unto you: Neither cower nor fear them.

  1. Numbers 14:9 Appeals against fear after the scouts return

    Moses tells the discouraged people not to fear the inhabitants; Joshua and Caleb likewise tell the congregation not to fear the people of the land because the Lord is among Israel.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 14:9 Only do not become deserters from the Lord. Do not fear the people of the land, for they are food for us. Their season has passed away from them, but the Lord is among us. Do not fear them.

    Pinned source record

Deuteronomy 1:30

The Lord your God, who goes before you, shall Himself fight against them with you, according to all that He did for you in the land of Egypt,

  1. Exodus 14:13-14 Egyptian deliverance as assurance that God fights

    Moses appeals to what the Lord did for Israel in Egypt as assurance that He will fight; the earlier sea-deliverance speech expressly promises that the Lord will fight for the frightened people.

    Read matching passage

    Exodus 14:13 And Moses said to the people, “Take courage; stand, and behold the salvation from God, which He shall accomplish for us today. For as you have seen the Egyptians today, you shall see them no more for ever.

    Exodus 14:14 The Lord shall fight for you, and you shall keep silence.”

    Pinned source record

Deuteronomy 1:31

and in this wilderness which you saw, how the Lord your God nurtured you as a man nurtures his son, in all the way which you went, until you came unto this place.

  1. Numbers 11:12 Parental and nursing imagery with different agents

    Moses says that God nurtured Israel like a father his son; his earlier complaint asks whether he gave birth to the people and must carry them as a nurse carries a child. The agents and metaphors remain different.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 11:12 Did I conceive all this people, or did I give birth unto them, that Thou sayest unto me, Carry them in your bosom, as a nurse carries a nursing child, unto the land which Thou didst swear unto their fathers?

    Pinned source record

Deuteronomy 1:32

And in this matter you did not trust the Lord your God,

  1. Numbers 14:11 Failure to believe despite witnessed divine acts

    Moses charges Israel with not trusting the Lord despite the preceding deliverance and nurture; Numbers records the Lord asking why the people refuse belief despite the signs performed among them.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 14:11 And the Lord said unto Moses: How long will this people provoke Me? How long will they refuse to believe Me, despite all the signs I have done among them?

    Pinned source record

Deuteronomy 1:33

who goes before you in the way to choose a place for you, guiding you in fire by night, to show you the way wherein you should go, and in a cloud by day.

  1. Exodus 13:21-22 Guidance by cloud and fire

    The Lord’s going before Israel by fire at night and cloud by day recalls the continuing pillars described in Exodus; Deuteronomy additionally specifies searching out camping places.

    Read matching passage

    Exodus 13:21 God led them by day in a pillar of cloud to show them the way, and by night in a pillar of fire.

    Exodus 13:22 The pillar of cloud by day and the pillar of fire by night did not cease before all the people.

    Pinned source record

Deuteronomy 1:34

And the Lord heard the voice of your words, and, being provoked to anger, sware, saying:

  1. Numbers 14:28-35 Angry oath concerning the resistant generation

    The Lord hears the people’s words and swears in anger; Numbers records the sworn announcement that the counted grumbling generation will die in the wilderness, with stated exceptions and provision for their children.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 14:28 Say to them, I live, says the Lord: I will certainly do to you just as you have spoken into My ears.

    Numbers 14:29 In this wilderness your bodies shall fall, with your entire registration and those of you counted from twenty years old and upward who grumbled against Me.

    Numbers 14:30 You shall certainly not enter the land over which I stretched out My hand to settle you upon it, except Caleb son of Jephunneh and Joshua son of Nun.

    Numbers 14:31 And I will bring into the land the children whom you said would become plunder, and they shall inherit the land from which you turned away.

    Numbers 14:32 And your bodies shall fall in this wilderness.

    Numbers 14:33 But your sons shall live as herders in the wilderness for forty years and bear your whoredom, until your bodies are consumed in the wilderness.

    Numbers 14:34 According to the number of days you searched out the land—forty days, a day for a year—you shall bear your sins for forty years, and you shall know the fury of My anger.

    Numbers 14:35 I, the Lord, have spoken. I will certainly do this to this evil congregation that has banded together against Me. In this wilderness they shall be consumed, and there they shall die.

    Pinned source record

Deuteronomy 1:35

Surely none of these men shall see this good land which I sware unto their fathers,

  1. Numbers 14:22-23 Provoking adults excluded from the sworn land

    The excluded generation will not see the good land sworn to the fathers; Numbers likewise excludes the men who saw the signs yet repeatedly tested God from seeing that sworn land.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 14:22 all the men who have seen My glory and the signs I did in Egypt and this wilderness, and have tested Me this tenth time and have not listened to My voice,

    Numbers 14:23 shall certainly not see the land I swore to their fathers. But their children who are here with Me, who do not know good or evil, every inexperienced young person—to these I will give the land. None of those who provoke Me shall see it.

    Pinned source record

Deuteronomy 1:36

save Caleb son of Jephunneh. He shall see it, and I will give unto him and unto his sons the land whereon he trod, because he cleaved unto the things of the Lord.

  1. Numbers 14:24 Caleb’s faithfulness and hereditary land grant

    Both passages single out Caleb for following the Lord and promise that he and his seed will possess the land he entered; the shared reason is explicitly stated faithfulness.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 14:24 But My servant Caleb, because a different spirit was in him and he followed Me, I will bring into the land he entered, and his seed shall inherit it.

    The command permits Moses to view the land but forbids crossing Jordan; the ending describes God showing him the land while excluding entry. Here the summit is Hewn Place; the ending names Nebo and Pisgah, without an added independent place-equivalence claim.

    Deuteronomy 34:3 and the wilderness, and the country round about Jericho, the city of palms, unto Zoar.

    Deuteronomy 34:4 And the Lord said unto Moses, This is the land which I sware unto Abraham, and Isaac, and Jacob, saying, I will give it unto your seed. And I have showed it unto your eyes, but you shall not go in thither.

    The fearful wilderness description includes biting serpents; Numbers narrates a particular serpent judgment and subsequent remedy, without claiming that every wilderness serpent mentioned here belongs to that episode.

    Read matching passage

    Numbers 11:2 And the people cried out unto Moses. Moses prayed unto the Lord, and the fire died down.

    Numbers 11:3 And that place was named Burning, because fire from the Lord had blazed among them.

  2. Exodus 17:1-7 Named Testing provocation and water dispute

    The recital names Testing; Exodus identifies Testing and Reviling after the people's thirsty dispute and question whether the Lord was among them.

    Read matching passage

    Exodus 17:1 The whole congregation of the sons of Israel set out from the wilderness of Sin by their encampments, at the Lord’s word, and camped at Rephidim. There was no water for the people to drink.

    Exodus 17:2 And the people reviled Moses, saying, “Give us water, that we may drink.” And Moses said to them, “Why do you revile me? And why do you test the Lord?”

    Exodus 17:3 And the people thirsted there for water, and murmured against Moses, saying, “Why have you brought us up out of Egypt, to kill us and our children and our livestock with thirst?”

    Exodus 17:4 Moses cried out to the Lord, “What shall I do with this people? A little more, and they will stone me to death!”

    Exodus 17:5 And the Lord said to Moses, “Go before this people, and take with you some of the elders of the people; and take in your hand the staff with which you struck the river, and go.

    Exodus 17:6 Behold, I stand before you there upon the rock in Horeb; and you shall strike the rock, and water shall come forth from it, and My people shall drink.” And Moses did so before the sons of Israel.

    Exodus 17:7 He named that place Testing and Reviling because the sons of Israel reviled and tested the Lord, saying, “Is the Lord among us or not?”

    Pinned source record
